# Proposed OEIS comments: A264252 to A268765

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A264252

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264252>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [384](../papers/00001-00500/00384-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 19*a(n-1) -84*a(n-2) -440*a(n-3) +4562*a(n-4) -6128*a(n-5) -52306*a(n-6) +176044*a(n-7) +178409*a(n-8) -1566967*a(n-9) +1071970*a(n-10) +6050538*a(n-11) -10528356*a(n-12) -8015378*a(n-13) +32496674*a(n-14) -11033682*a(n-15) -40764983*a(n-16) +40764983*a(n-17) +11033682*a(n-18) -32496674*a(n-19) +8015378*a(n-20) +10528356*a(n-21) -6050538*a(n-22) -1071970*a(n-23) +1566967*a(n-24) -178409*a(n-25) -176044*a(n-26) +52306*a(n-27) +6128*a(n-28) -4562*a(n-29) +440*a(n-30) +84*a(n-31) -19*a(n-32) +a(n-33)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 31. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264268

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264268>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [271](../papers/00001-00500/00271-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 52 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 52, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264509

Paper: [219](../papers/00001-00500/00219-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 69 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264569>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2561](../papers/02501-03000/02561-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:
> Empirical for row n:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrences for columns k = 1, 2 and 4 are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264572

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264572>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [260](../papers/00001-00500/00260-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 56 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 56, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 - x + 7*x^2 + x^4 + 4*x^5 - 2*x^6 - x^8) / (1 - 3*x + 3*x^2 - 5*x^3 - 7*x^4 + 5*x^5 - 7*x^6 + 3*x^7 - x^8 + x^9). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 08 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 4 096 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 9. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A265922

Result obtained: **7 September 2026**

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) +110*a(n-2) +186*a(n-3) -1189*a(n-4) -2127*a(n-5) +4920*a(n-6) +5776*a(n-7) -9216*a(n-8)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- one bit per column where it asks for a parity reaching back to the top, and otherwise a window of three consecutive lines, since every cell it names lies one step away -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 11 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A265967

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A265967>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [5229](../papers/05001-05500/05229-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 8*a(n-2) +76*a(n-3) +219*a(n-4) -150*a(n-5) -2179*a(n-6) -6990*a(n-7) -7878*a(n-8) +14114*a(n-9) +97811*a(n-10) +209622*a(n-11) +139056*a(n-12) -409764*a(n-13) -1836900*a(n-14) -2835340*a(n-15) -1654712*a(n-16) +6180672*a(n-17) +17827872*a(n-18) +20705808*a(n-19) +4962208*a(n-20) -56431488*a(n-21) -79309568*a(n-22) -99599360*a(n-23) +85772288*a(n-24) +144689152*a(n-25) +353329152*a(n-26) -42172416*a(n-27) -139067392*a(n-28) -541589504*a(n-29) +2097152*a(n-30) +25165824*a(n-31) +301989888*a(n-32) for n>33

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- one bit per column where it asks for a parity reaching back to the top, and otherwise a window of three consecutive lines, since every cell it names lies one step away -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 47 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 33. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```

## A266063

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A266063>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10970](../papers/10501-11000/10970-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 78 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 97 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 78, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A267638

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267638>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [2896](../papers/02501-03000/02896-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) +6*a(n-2) -6*a(n-3) -8*a(n-4) +8*a(n-5).
> Empirical: G.f.: -2*x*(1+3*x-x^2-2*x^3+2*x^4) / ( (x-1)*(2*x+1)*(2*x-1)*(2*x^2-1) ). - _R. J. Mathar_, Jan 26 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 40 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A267641

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267641>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10830](../papers/10501-11000/10830-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 93 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 2619 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 93, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A267790

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267790>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10833](../papers/10501-11000/10833-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 93 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 589 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 93, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1688 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 56, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> For integer r and positive integer s, define sequences {u(n) : n >= 0} and {v(n) : n >= 0} by setting u(n) = [x^(s*n)] F(x)^(r*n) and v(n) = [x^(s*n)] E(x)^(r*n). We conjecture that both u(n) and v(n) satisfy the above supercongruences. (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A268623

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268623>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [3157](../papers/03001-03500/03157-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 10*a(n-1) - 29*a(n-2) + 20*a(n-3) - 4*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: 6*x*(4 - 11*x + 7*x^2 + x^3) / (1 - 5*x + 2*x^2)^2. - _Colin Barker_, Jan 14 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 64 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
